Appendix A
Generators of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}

In this section, we provide an explicit description of the group of symmetries
G_{GTVW}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
of the K3 model
\mathcal{C}_{GTVW}
considered in section
5
; see
[
69
]
and
[
70
]
for a derivation. Recall that the full symmetry group of the CFT is
(SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}):S_{6}
, where the
SU(2)^{6}
factors are generated by the zero modes of the holomorphic and antiholomorphic currents, while
G_{GTVW}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
is the subgroup that preserves the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
superconformal algebra and the spectral flow. Every element of
(SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}):S_{6}
can be written as
(A_{1},\ldots,A_{6}\ B_{1},\ldots,B_{6})\pi
where
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
are
SU(2)
matrices, and
\pi\in S_{6}
is a permutation. As discussed in section
5
,
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
does not act faithfully on the operators of the CFT. There is a subgroup
Z_{0}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{5}
of the center
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{6}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{6}
of
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
that acts trivially on all states of the theory (see eq.(
5.9
)). This means that there is an ambiguity in writing the symmetries as
(A_{1},\ldots,A_{6}\ B_{1},\ldots,B_{6})\pi
, as one can multiply by any element in
Z_{0}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{5}
.

The
SU(2)
matrices that one needs to write all elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
are

(A.1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad x=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad y=\begin{pmatrix}0&i\\
i&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad z=\begin{pmatrix}i&0\\
0&-i\end{pmatrix}

and their opposite matrices

(A.2)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathsf{1}}=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad\underline{x}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad\underline{y}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-i\\
-i&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad\underline{z}=\begin{pmatrix}-i&0\\
0&i\end{pmatrix}\ .

Notice that
x,y,z
obey the quaternionic relations
x^{2}=y^{2}=z^{2}=-1
,
xy=z=-yx
. Finally we need the matrices

(A.3)

\displaystyle\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1-i}{2}&\frac{1+i}{2}\\
-\frac{1-i}{2}&\frac{1+i}{2}\end{pmatrix}\ ,\qquad\Omega^{{\dagger}}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1+i}{2}&\frac{1+i}{2}\\
\frac{1-i}{2}&\frac{1-i}{2}\end{pmatrix}

that satisfy
\Omega^{3}=-\mathsf{1}
, so that
\Omega^{6}=\mathsf{1}
and
\Omega^{\dagger}=-\Omega^{2}
. Furthermore,
\Omega x\Omega^{\dagger}=z
,
\Omega y\Omega^{\dagger}=x
,
\Omega z\Omega^{\dagger}=y
. The permutations
\pi
will be denoted by their non-trivial cycles.

In this notation, the generators of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
are as follows:

•

Generators in the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{6}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{6}
subgroup of
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
:

(A.4)

\displaystyle t_{23}=(\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\ \mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

(A.5)

\displaystyle t_{24}=(\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\ \mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

(A.6)

\displaystyle t_{25}=(\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\ \mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

(A.7)

\displaystyle t_{26}=(\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\underline{\mathsf{1}}\ \mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1}\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

These generators form a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}
group, with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
, that contains
10
elements
t_{ij}
,
2\leq i<j\leq 6
with non-trivial
SU(2)
factors
A_{i}=A_{j}=\underline{\mathsf{1}}
, and
5
elements
t_{ijkl}
,
2\leq i<j<k<l\leq 6
, with non-trivial
SU(2)
factors
A_{i}=A_{j}=A_{k}=A_{l}=\underline{\mathsf{1}}
.

•

Further generators of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}
normal subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}

(A.8)

\displaystyle s_{1}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{x}\,\underline{x}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,x\,x\,x\,x)(1)^{6}

(A.9)

\displaystyle s_{2}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\underline{y}\,\underline{y}\,\underline{y}\,\underline{y}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,y\,y\,y\,y)(1)^{6}

(A.10)

\displaystyle s_{3}=(\mathsf{1}\,x\,z\,\underline{y}\,\underline{x}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,x\,z\,y\,x\,\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

(A.11)

\displaystyle s_{4}=(\mathsf{1}\,z\,y\,\underline{x}\,\underline{z}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,z\,y\,x\,z\,\mathsf{1})(1)^{6}

•

Generators of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}
subgroup of
M_{20}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}.A_{5}
(modulo elements of the form
t_{ijkl}
)

(A.12)

\displaystyle v_{1}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,x\,x\,x\,x\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,z\,z\,z\,z)(1)^{6}

(A.13)

\displaystyle v_{2}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,y\,y\,y\,y\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,x\,x\,x\,x)(1)^{6}

(A.14)

\displaystyle v_{3}=(\mathsf{1}\,y\,y\,\mathsf{1}\,x\,z\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,x\,x\,\mathsf{1}\,z\,y)(1)^{6}

(A.15)

\displaystyle v_{4}=(\mathsf{1}\,x\,y\,z\,\mathsf{1}\,x\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,z\,x\,y\,\mathsf{1}\,z)(1)^{6}

Altogether, the
t_{ij}
,
s_{1},\ldots,s_{4}
, and
v_{1},\ldots,v_{4}
generate the subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
contained in
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
.

•

Generators of the quotient
A_{5}
of
M_{20}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}.A_{5}

(A.16)

\displaystyle p_{1}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(34)(56)

(A.17)

\displaystyle p_{2}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(35)(46)

(A.18)

\displaystyle p_{3}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\Omega\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\Omega\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(25)(34)

Action on the currents.
The
18
left-moving currents of
su(2)^{6}
can be denoted by a pair
(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n},i)_{L}
, where
i=1,\ldots,6
denotes the
su(2)_{1}
factors, and
\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n}
is a traceless hermitian matrix, written a linear combination of Pauli matrices with coefficients
\vec{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\vec{n}\cdot\vec{n}=1
. The action of
g=(A_{,}\ldots,A_{6};B_{1},\ldots,B_{6})\pi
on
(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n},i)_{L}
is

(A.19)

\displaystyle g\cdot(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n},i)_{L}=(A_{\pi(i)}\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n}A_{\pi(i)}^{\dagger},\pi(i))_{L}

where we make the permutation act before the
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
adjoint action. Similarly, the action on the right-moving currents is

(A.20)

\displaystyle g\cdot(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n},i)_{R}=(B_{\pi(i)}\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{n}B_{\pi(i)}^{\dagger},\pi(i))_{R}\ .

Action on the RR ground states.
An orthonormal basis of the
24
-dimensional space of RR ground states is given by six ‘tetrads’ of states
|1,i\rangle
,
|2,i\rangle
,
|3,i\rangle
,
|4,i\rangle
,
i=1,\ldots,6
. One can assign with each element
|a,i\rangle
in a tetrad a
2\times 2
matrix
U_{a}
,
a=1,2,3,4
, as follows

(A.21)

\displaystyle|1,i\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}=\frac{x}{\sqrt{2}}

(A.22)

\displaystyle|2,i\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\frac{\mathsf{1}}{\sqrt{2}}

(A.23)

\displaystyle|3,i\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{3}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}i&0\\
0&-i\end{pmatrix}=-\frac{z}{\sqrt{2}}

(A.24)

\displaystyle|4,i\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U_{4}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&i\\
i&0\end{pmatrix}=\frac{y}{\sqrt{2}}\ ,

in such a way that
\langle a,i|b,j\rangle=\delta_{ij}\Tr(U_{a}^{\dagger}U_{b})
. The action of
g=(A_{1},\ldots,A_{6};B_{1},\ldots,B_{6})\pi
on
|a,i\rangle\equiv(U_{a},i)
,
a=1,\ldots,4
,
i=1,\ldots,6
is

(A.25)

\displaystyle g\cdot(U_{a},i)=(A_{\pi(i)}U_{a}B_{\pi(i)}^{t},\pi(i))\ .

Appendix B
A duality defect not in
\mathsf{Top}_{GTVW}

Consider the K3 model
\mathcal{C}_{GTVW}
described in section
5
and let
H\subset G_{GTVW}
the abelian group
H\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
generated by the symmetry
g=t_{5}t_{6}
.
By computing its partition function, one can show that the orbifold
\mathcal{C}/H
is a consistent K3 model with the same bosonic chiral and anti-chiral algebras and representations as
\mathcal{C}
; therefore, the two CFTs must be isomorphic
\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}/H
. In particular, all holomorphic and anti-holomorphic currents are
g
-invariant; the
g
-twisted sector contains no further currents.
Thus, we expect a topological duality defect
\mathcal{N}
such that

(B.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{2}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{g}\ ,\qquad{\cal L}_{g}\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}{\cal L}_{g}=\mathcal{N}\ .

In this section, we describe the duality defect
\mathcal{N}
and show that
\mathcal{N}\not\in\mathsf{Top}_{GTVW}
, and in particular that
\mathcal{N}
does not preserve the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
superconformal algebra.

The fusion rules for
\mathcal{N}\equiv\mathcal{N}_{56}
imply that
\hat{\mathcal{N}}
must act with eigenvalues
\pm\sqrt{2}
on the states that are fixed by
g
, while eigenstates of
g
with eigenvalue
-1
must be in the kernel. On the other hand, these topological defects
\mathcal{N}
cannot commute with the whole
(\widehat{su}(2)_{1})^{\oplus 6}
algebra – we have already identified all such defects, and they are all invertible.

What is the possible action on the chiral algebra?

•

Consider moving a defect
\mathcal{N}
through local holomorphic operators in
{\cal A}
, we obtain a map from
{\cal A}
to itself in a way compatible with OPE. This means that the defect should act on
{\cal A}
by an automorphism of order
2
.

•

The action of
\mathcal{N}
on
{\cal A}
should not lift to a symmetry of the whole CFT. Indeed, if this was the case, then for a suitable CFT symmetry
h
, the fusion
{\cal L}_{h}\mathcal{N}
would act trivially on
{\cal A}
. But the only simple defects with this property are the Verlinde lines for the algebra
{\cal A}
and all such defects are invertible. It would follow that
\mathcal{N}
is a superposition of invertible defects, which cannot be true for a duality defect.

•

Because
\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{2}
acts trivially on the
g
-invariant space of states
\mathcal{H}^{g}
, then
\mathcal{N}
acts by permutations on the set of
{\cal A}\times\bar{\cal A}
representations contained in
\mathcal{H}^{g}
. In particular, it cannot map any such representation into one that is not contained in
\mathcal{H}^{g}
.

The group of outer automorphisms of the chiral and antichiral algebra is the
S_{6}\times S_{6}
permutation group acting separately on the holomophic and anti-holomorphic currents. Only the diagonal
S_{6}\subset S_{6}\times S_{6}
lifts to a symmetry of the whole theory; the other elements of do not preserve the set of
{\cal A}\times\bar{\cal A}
representations in the space of states
\mathcal{H}
. If instead we consider the subgroup of
S_{6}\times S_{6}
that preserve only the set of representations contained in the subspace
\mathcal{H}^{g}\subset\mathcal{H}
of
g
-invariant states, then there is one non-trivial choice (modulo the diagonal
S_{6}
symmetry): the involution exchanging the
5
-th and the
6
-th
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
components on the (say) holomorphic side, while keeping the anti-holomorphic side fixed.

Therefore, modulo automorphisms that lift to CFT symmetries, this is the only possible action of
\mathcal{N}
on the algebra
(\widehat{su}(2)_{1})^{6}
.

We can calculate the
\mathcal{N}
-twined partition functions

(B.2)

\displaystyle Z^{NSNS}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\Tr_{NS-NS}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})\ ,\qquad\tilde{Z}^{NSNS}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\Tr_{NS-NS}((-1)^{F+\tilde{F}}\hat{\mathcal{N}}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})

(B.3)

\displaystyle Z^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\Tr_{R-R}(\hat{\mathcal{N}}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})\ ,\qquad\tilde{Z}^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\Tr_{R-R}((-1)^{F+\tilde{F}}\hat{\mathcal{N}}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})

using the characters of
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
, namely

(B.4)

\displaystyle{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,z)=\frac{\theta_{3}(2\tau,2z)}{\eta(\tau)}\ ,\qquad{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,z)=\frac{\theta_{2}(2\tau,2z)}{\eta(\tau)}\ ,

from which

(B.5)

\displaystyle{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)=\frac{\theta_{3}(2\tau,0)}{\eta(\tau)}=\frac{\eta(2\tau)^{5}}{\eta(\tau)^{3}\eta(4\tau)^{2}}\ ,\qquad{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)=\frac{\theta_{2}(2\tau,0)}{\eta(\tau)}=2\frac{\eta(4\tau)^{2}}{\eta(2\tau)\eta(\tau)}\ .

The S-transformations are

(B.6)

\displaystyle{\rm ch}_{1,0}(-\tfrac{1}{\tau},0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)\bigr)\ ,\quad{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(-\tfrac{1}{\tau},0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)-{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)\bigr)

Recall
\hat{\mathcal{N}}
acts on the
t_{5}t_{6}
invariant states by
\sqrt{2}
times the permutation of the fifth and sixth holomorphic
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
components.
It follows that the
\mathcal{N}
-twined partition function are

\displaystyle Z^{NSNS}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}\\
\sum a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigl[\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left|{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\right|^{2}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\prod_{i=1}^{4}{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}-a_{i}}(\tau,0)}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{NSNS}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}\\
\sum a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigl[\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left|{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\right|^{2}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle-\prod_{i=1}^{4}{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}-a_{i}}(\tau,0)}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle Z^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}\\
\sum a_{i}\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigl[\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left|{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\right|^{2}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\prod_{i=1}^{4}{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}-a_{i}}(\tau,0)}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}\\
\sum a_{i}\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Bigl[\prod_{i=1}^{4}\left|{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\right|^{2}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle-\prod_{i=1}^{4}{\rm ch}_{1,a_{i}}(\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}-a_{i}}(\tau,0)}\bigl({\rm ch}_{1,0}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(\tau,0)}^{2}+{\rm ch}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}(2\tau,0)\overline{{\rm ch}_{1,0}(\tau,0)}^{2}\bigr)\Bigr]

By a direct calculation we obtain

(B.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{Z}^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}=(16+36q+96q^{2}+\ldots)+O(\bar{q}^{1})\ .

This shows that
\mathcal{N}
does not preserve any holomorphic supercurrent. Indeed, if a holomorphic supercurrent were preserved by
\mathcal{N}
, then
\tilde{Z}^{RR}_{1,\mathcal{N}}
would receive contributions only from the RR ground states, and it would be a constant in
q
.

Appendix C
Defects acting by automorphisms of the chiral algebra

In this section we classify the topological defects
{\cal L}\in\mathsf{Top}_{GTVW}
of the model
\mathcal{C}_{GTVW}
of section
5
, such that
\hat{\cal L}
acts on all holomorphic fields generating the bosonic chiral algebra
{\cal A}\cong(\widehat{su}(2)_{1})^{6}
by an algebra automorphism
\rho_{L}
times the quantum dimension
\langle{\cal L}\rangle
. This implies that when a defect line
{\cal L}
is moved past the insertion point of one of the holomorphic currents
j(z)
, the latter gets simply transformed into the current
\rho_{L}(j(z))
. Similarly, we require the defect to act on the anti-holomorphic fields by a (possibly different) automorphism
\rho_{R}
. The group of automorphisms of
(\widehat{su}(2)_{1})^{6}
is
SO(3)^{6}\rtimes S_{6}
, where
SO(3)^{6}
are the inner automorphisms generated by the zero modes of the currents; note that the center
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{6}
of
SU(2)^{6}
acts trivially on the algebra itself.
We still require
\rho_{L}
and
\rho_{R}
to act trivially on the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
superconformal algebra; this condition constrains
\rho_{L}
and
\rho_{R}
to be in a finite subgroup of
SO(3)^{6}\rtimes S_{6}
. If the automorphisms
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
extends to a symmetry of the CFT, then there is an invertible defect
{\cal L}_{g}
, for some
g\in G_{GTVW}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
, such that the fusion
{\cal L}_{g}{\cal L}
acts trivially on the whole
{\cal A}\otimes\bar{\cal A}
. The only simple defects with this property are the Verlinde lines discussed in section
5.1
, where it is shown that they are all invertible. This means that, in this case,
{\cal L}
is just a superposition of invertible defects.

Therefore, in order to get some new defects, we have to require that
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
does
not
lift to a symmetry of the CFT. Now, inner automorphisms
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
are generated by the current zero modes, and therefore always define CFT symmetries. The group of outer automorphisms of
{\cal A}\times\bar{\cal A}
is
S_{6}\times S_{6}
, and in this case, only the diagonal
S_{6}^{diag}\subset S_{6}\times S_{6}
, permuting the holomorphic and antiholomorphic
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
factors in the same way, lifts to a CFT symmetry.
Thus, the defects acting by algebra automorphisms, modulo fusion with invertible defects from the left or from the right, correspond to non-trivial double cosets in

(C.1)

\displaystyle\Bigl((SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6})\rtimes S_{6}^{diag}\Bigr)\backslash\Bigl((SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6})\rtimes(S_{6}\times S_{6})\Bigr)/\Bigl((SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6})\rtimes S_{6}^{diag}\Bigr)\ .

For each such coset, we can always choose a representative
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
with
\rho_{R}=1
. Furthermore, we can choose one
\rho_{L}
for each
S_{6}
-conjugacy class, i.e. for each possible cycle shape.

Finally, we require
\rho_{L}
to preserve the
\mathcal{N}=4
superconformal algebra and the spectral flow generators. This implies that the induced permutation must be contained in the
A_{5}\subset S_{6}
subgroup of
even
permutations fixing the first
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
factor. There are only three possible non-trivial cycle shapes, corresponding to the partitions
1+1+1+3
,
1+1+2+2
and
1+5
. Let us consider each of these three cases in detail.

Partition
1+1+1+3
.
It is known (see appendix
A
), that
G_{GTVW}
contains a symmetry

(C.2)

\displaystyle g_{3}=p_{1}\circ p_{3}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(265)\ ,

where we denote the elements of
(SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6})\rtimes S_{6}^{diag}
by
(A_{1},\ldots,A_{6}\,;B_{1},\ldots,B_{6})\pi
, with
A_{i},B_{i}\in SU(2)
and
\pi\in S_{6}^{diag}
, and where

(C.3)

\displaystyle\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1-i}{2}&\frac{1+i}{2}\\
-\frac{1-i}{2}&\frac{1+i}{2}\end{pmatrix}\in SU(2)\ .

This means that if we define
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
as

\rho_{L}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(265)\ ,\qquad\rho_{R}=1\ .

the left automorphism
\rho_{L}
acts on the holomorphic algebra in the same way as the symmetry element
g_{3}
. This ensures that the holomorphic
\mathcal{N}=4
supercurrents are invariant under
\rho_{L}
. However,
\rho_{R}
is not the same as for
g_{3}
, and in particular the left- and right-moving
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}
factors are permuted in different ways. This means that the pair
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
cannot be extended to a symmetry of the whole CFT.

More explicitly, any such symmetry would have to map any field in the representation
[110000;110000]
, for example, to a field in a representation
[100010;110000]
; however, while the former field is in the spectrum of the theory, the latter is not. This implies that there cannot be any
invertible
defect acting by
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
on the chiral and antichiral algebras. However, there is no obstruction to having a non-invertible defect with such an action. Indeed, a non-invertible defect
{\cal L}
, when circling a field in the
[110000;110000]
representation, can simply annihilate it. More generally, the operator
\hat{\cal L}
associated with any such defect needs to annihilate any field in a representation
[a_{1}\ldots,a_{6};b_{1}\ldots b_{6}]
such that
[\rho_{L}(a_{1}\ldots,a_{6});\rho_{R}(b_{1}\ldots b_{6})]
is not in the spectrum. The NS-NS representations
[a_{1}\ldots,a_{6};b_{1}\ldots b_{6}]
that are not necessarily annihilated by
{\cal L}
are the ones satisfying

(C.4)

\displaystyle a_{2}=a_{5}=a_{6}\ ,\qquad b_{2}=b_{5}=b_{6}\ ,

and can be grouped into four sets:

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\{[000000;000000],\ [111111;000000],\ [000000;111111],\ [111111;111111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\{[101000;101000],\ [010111;101000],\ [101000;010111],\ [010111;010111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=\{[100100;100100],\ [011011;100100],\ [100100;011011],\ [011011;011011]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{4}

\displaystyle=\{[001100;001100],\ [110011;001100],\ [001100;110011],\ [110011;110011]\}\ .

The four members in each set are related to each other by the action of the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
supercurrents. Therefore, the requirement that the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
algebra is invariant under
\hat{\cal L}
implies that the action of
\hat{\cal L}
is the same on all representations in the same set
\Omega_{i}
. We conclude that the action of
\hat{\cal L}
on the NS-NS sector depends only on four parameters
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{4}
as

(C.5)

\displaystyle\hat{\cal L}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}P^{i}_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}\ .

Here, for each
i=1,\ldots,4
,
P^{i}_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}
acts by the automorphism
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
on the representations in the set
\Omega_{i}
, while it annihilates all fields in the representations in
\Omega_{j}
for
j\neq i
.

Requiring
{\cal L}
to commute with the spectral flow operators fixes the action of
\hat{\cal L}
on the R-R sector to be of the same form (
C.5
) with the same parameters
\alpha_{i}
, where now each
P^{i}_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}
is non-zero only on the set of representations
\tilde{\Omega}_{i}
with

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{[100000;100000],\ [011111;100000],\ [100000;011111],\ [011111;011111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{[001000;001000],\ [110111;001000],\ [001000;110111],\ [110111;110111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{3}

\displaystyle=\{[000100;000100],\ [111011;000100],\ [000100;111011],\ [111011;111011]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{4}

\displaystyle=\{[101100;101100],\ [010011;101100],\ [101100;010011],\ [010011;010011]\}\ .

The four parameters
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{4}
are constrained by the Cardy-like conditions that are obtained by considering the torus partition function with the defect line
{\cal L}
wrapping one of the cycles. Notice that if
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
correspond to a consistent defect
{\cal L}
acting by
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
on the chiral and antichiral algebras, then also
(\alpha_{1},-\alpha_{2},-\alpha_{3},\alpha_{4})
,
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},-\alpha_{3},-\alpha_{4})
, and
(\alpha_{1},-\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3},-\alpha_{4})
correspond to consistent defects, since they can be obtained by fusion of
{\cal L}
with the invertible defects acting trivially on all the currents and all the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
supercurrents (in particular, fusion with the symmetries
t_{2}t_{3}
,
t_{2}t_{4}
and
t_{3}t_{4}
gives all such defects; fusion with the symmetries
t_{2}t_{5}
,
t_{2}t_{6}
, and
t_{5}t_{6}
leaves each of these defects invariant).

Let us consider the possible fusion products of the simple defect
{\cal L}
with
\alpha_{1}=\ldots=\alpha_{4}
. The dual defect
{\cal L}^{\star}
acts on the space of states by the adjoint operator

(C.6)

\displaystyle\hat{\cal L}^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}^{*}P^{i}_{\rho_{L}^{-1},\rho_{R}^{-1}}

so that
the product
{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}
acts by

(C.7)

\displaystyle\hat{\cal L}\hat{\cal L}^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}|\alpha_{i}|^{2}P^{i}\ ,

where
P^{i}
is the projector on the representations in the set
\Omega_{i}
. On the other hand, we know that
{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal I}+\ldots
acts trivially on the whole algebra
\widehat{su}(2)_{1}^{6}\oplus\widehat{su}(2)_{1}^{6}
as well as on the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
algebra. This means that it must be a superposition of the invertible defects
{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}}
generating the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
that is contained in the centre of
SU(2)^{6}\times SU(2)^{6}
. Furthermore,
{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}
must annihilate the representations
[a_{1},\ldots,b_{6}]
of the chiral algebra that do not satisfy (
C.4
), and must be a sum with positive coefficients of the projectors
P_{i}
. The only possibility is

(C.8)

\displaystyle{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{5}}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{6}}+{\cal L}_{t_{5}t_{6}}\ ,

so that

|\alpha_{i}|=\langle{\cal L}\rangle=\langle{\cal L}^{\star}\rangle=2\ .

The torus partition function with the insertion of
{\cal L}
is

(C.9)

\displaystyle Z_{{\cal L}}(\tau)=\Tr(\hat{\cal L}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}\Tr(P_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}^{i}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})\ ,

where

\displaystyle\Tr(P_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}^{i}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})=\sum_{[a_{1},\ldots,b_{6}]\in\Omega_{i}}\Bigl[

\displaystyle\ch_{a_{1}}(\tau,0)\ch_{a_{2}}(3\tau,0)\ch_{a_{3}}(\tau,\frac{1}{6})\ch_{a_{4}}(\tau,\frac{1}{6})

\displaystyle\overline{\bigl(\ch_{b_{1}}(\tau,0)\ch_{b_{2}}(\tau,0)^{3}\ch_{b_{3}}(\tau,0)\ch_{b_{4}}(\tau,0)\bigr)}\Bigr]

Let us calculate
Z_{\cal L}
with the ansatz

(C.10)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4}=\langle{\cal L}\rangle

Using the
su(2)
characters,
Z_{\mathcal{L}}(\tau)
takes the form:

\begin{split}Z_{\mathcal{L}}(\tau)&=\frac{\langle{\cal L}\rangle}{\overline{\eta(\tau)^{6}}\eta(\tau)^{3}\eta(3\tau)}\left\{\left(\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{6}}+\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{6}}\right)\left[\theta_{3}(2\tau)\theta_{3}(6\tau)\theta_{3}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)^{2}+\theta_{2}(2\tau)\theta_{2}(6\tau)\theta_{2}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)^{2}\right]+\right.\\
&+\left.\left(\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}}\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{4}}+\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}}\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{4}}\right)\left[2\left(\theta_{3}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)\theta_{2}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)\right)\left(\theta_{3}(2\tau)\theta_{2}(6\tau)+\theta_{2}(2\tau)\theta_{3}(6\tau)\right)+\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.+\left(\theta_{3}(2\tau)\theta_{3}(6\tau)\theta_{2}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)^{2}+\theta_{2}(2\tau)\theta_{2}(6\tau)\theta_{3}\left(2\tau,\frac{1}{3}\right)^{2}\right)\right]\right\}.\\
\end{split}

Applying the transformation
\tau\mapsto-1/\tau
we get:

\begin{split}&Z^{\mathcal{L}}(\tau)=\frac{\langle{\cal L}\rangle q^{1/18}}{32\overline{\eta(\tau)^{6}}\eta(\tau)^{3}\eta(\tau/3)}\left\{\left[\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)^{2}+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)^{2}\right)\right.+\right.\\
&\left.\left.+2\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\right)\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\right)\right]\left(\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{4}}\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}}+\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{4}}\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}}\right)+\right.\\
&\left.+\left[\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)^{2}+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{6}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2};\frac{\tau}{6}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{6}}+\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{6}}\right)\right\},\\
\end{split}

which series expansion in
q
and
\bar{q}
is:

\begin{split}Z^{\mathcal{L}}=&\frac{\langle{\cal L}\rangle}{q^{1/4}\overline{q}^{1/4}}\left[\left(\frac{q^{1/6}}{2}+\frac{3}{2}q^{1/2}+2q^{2/3}+3q^{5/6}+6q+9q^{7/6}+12q^{4/3}+\frac{39}{2}q^{3/2}+28q^{5/3}+36q^{11/6}+\right.\right.\\
&\left.+54q^{2}+\frac{149}{2}q^{13/6}+96q^{7/3}+\frac{273}{2}q^{5/2}+182q^{8/3}+234q^{17/6}+O\left(q^{3}\right)\right)+\bar{q}^{1/2}\left(6q^{1/6}+40q^{1/3}+\right.\\
&\left.+82q^{1/2}+128q^{2/3}+196q^{5/6}+320q+524q^{7/6}+776q^{4/3}+1098q^{3/2}+1616q^{5/3}+2320q^{11/6}+\right.\\
&\left.\left.+O\left(q^{2}\right)\right)+\bar{q}\left(33q^{1/6}+160q^{1/3}+355q^{1/2}+548q^{2/3}+838q^{5/6}+O\left(q\right)\right)+O\left(\overline{q}^{3/2}\right)\right].\\
\end{split}

As predicted above, we find that such expansion produces only integer non-negative coefficients if
\langle{\cal L}\rangle=2
.

Notice that the automorphism
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
has order
3
, so that
{\cal L}^{2}
acts on the chiral algebra by
(\rho_{L}^{2},\rho_{R}^{2})=(\rho_{L}^{-1},\rho_{R}^{-1})
(the same as
{\cal L}^{\star}
), while
{\cal L}^{3}
acts trivially on the chiral algebra. This suggests

(C.11)

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{2}=2{\cal L}^{\star}\ ,

and

(C.12)

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{3}=2{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}=2({\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{5}}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{6}}+{\cal L}_{t_{5}t_{6}})\ ,

which fits with the quantum dimensions.

We notice that if
{\cal L}_{p_{1}}
is the invertible defect corresponding to the symmetry

p_{1}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(34)(56)

of order
2
, then
{\cal L}_{p_{1}}{\cal L}{\cal L}_{p_{1}}
acts by
(\rho_{L}^{-1},\rho_{R}^{-1})
on the chiral algebra. This suggests that

(C.13)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{p_{1}}{\cal L}{\cal L}_{p_{1}}={\cal L}^{\star}\ .

As a consequence, if we define

(C.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{256}:={\cal L}{\cal L}_{p_{1}}\ ,

then
\mathcal{N}_{256}
is unoriented
\mathcal{N}_{256}=\mathcal{N}_{256}^{\star}
, and

(C.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{256}^{2}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{5}}+{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{6}}+{\cal L}_{t_{5}t_{6}}\ .

This means that
\mathcal{N}_{256}
is the duality defect related to the fact that the theory is self-orbifold with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
group of symmetries with generators
t_{2}t_{5}
and
t_{2}t_{6}
. Similarly, by conjugating by invertible defects, for every
1<i<j<k\leq 6
one can find duality defects
\mathcal{N}_{ijk}
of order
2
such that

(C.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{ijk}^{2}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}}+{\cal L}_{t_{j}t_{k}}+{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{k}}\ .

Partition
1+1+2+2
.
Let us now consider the class of defects acting by

(C.17)

\displaystyle\rho_{L}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(34)(56)\ ,\qquad\rho_{R}=1\ ,

on the chiral and anti-chiral algebra. We denote such defects by
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{a}
,
a=1,2,\ldots
. Because
\rho_{L}
act in the same way as
p_{1}\in G_{GTVW}
on the chiral algebra,
\hat{{\cal L}}^{(34)(56)}_{a}
must preserve the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
SCA. The only representations where
\hat{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{a}
can be non-zero are the ones satisfying

(C.18)

\displaystyle a_{3}=a_{4}\ ,\qquad a_{5}=a_{6}\ ,\qquad b_{3}=b_{4}\ ,\qquad b_{5}=b_{6}\ .

Once again, we arrange such representations (in the NS-NS sector) in sets

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\{[000000;000000],\ [111111;000000],\ [000000;111111],\ [111111;111111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\{[110000;110000],\ [001111;110000],\ [110000;001111],\ [001111;001111]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=\{[001100;001100],\ [110011;001100],\ [001100;110011],\ [110011;110011]\}\ ,

\displaystyle\Omega_{4}

\displaystyle=\{[000011;000011],\ [111100;000011],\ [000011;111100],\ [111100;111100]\}\ ,

so that

\hat{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{a}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}P^{i}_{(34)(56)}\ .

Because there are
4
parameters
\alpha_{i}
, we expect (at most)
4
simple defects of this kind. Let us denote by
{\cal L}:={\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{1}
one of these defects. Then, by fusion of
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}
with the invertible defects
{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{3}}
,
{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{5}}
,
{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{5}}
, we find three more simple defects
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{2}
,
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{3}
,
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}_{4}
whose parameters
\alpha_{i}
differ only by signs. The product
{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal L}^{\star}{\cal L}={\cal I}+\ldots
acts trivially on the whole chiral and antichiral algebra, as well as on the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
superconformal algebra, and annihilate the representations that do not satisfy (
C.18
). This leads to

(C.19)

\displaystyle{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{4}}+{\cal L}_{t_{5}t_{6}}+{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{4}t_{5}t_{6}}\ .

It follows that

(C.20)

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}|=|\alpha_{2}|=|\alpha_{3}|=|\alpha_{4}|=|{\cal L}|=2\ .

The torus partition function is

Z_{{\cal L}}(\tau)=\Tr(\hat{\cal L}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}\Tr(P_{\rho_{L},\rho_{R}}^{i}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})

where

\displaystyle\Tr(P_{(34)(56)}^{i}q^{L_{0}-\frac{c}{24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-\frac{\bar{c}}{24}})=\sum_{[a_{1},\ldots,b_{6}]\in\Omega_{i}}\Bigl[

\displaystyle\ch_{a_{1}}(\tau,0)\ch_{a_{2}}(\tau,0)\ch_{a_{3}}(2\tau,0)\ch_{a_{5}}(2\tau,0)

\displaystyle\overline{\bigl(\ch_{b_{1}}(\tau,0)\ch_{b_{2}}(\tau,0)\ch_{b_{3}}(\tau,0)^{2}\ch_{b_{5}}(\tau,0)^{2}\bigr)}\Bigr]

Using the ansatz
\alpha_{i}=\langle\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}\rangle
, the partition function with a
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}
insertion can be written in terms of the Theta functions as:

\begin{split}Z_{\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}}(\tau)&=\frac{\langle{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}\rangle}{\overline{\eta(\tau)^{6}}\eta(\tau)^{2}\eta(2\tau)^{2}}\left\{\left(\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{6}}+\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{6}}\right)\left(\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{3}(4\tau)^{2}+\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{2}(4\tau)^{2}\right)+\right.\\
&\left.+\left(\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}}\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{4}}+\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}}\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{4}}\right)\left(\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{3}(4\tau)^{2}+\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{2}(4\tau)^{2}\right)+\right.\\
&\left.+2\left(\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{4}}\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}}+\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}}\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{4}}\right)\left(\theta_{3}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{3}(4\tau)\theta_{2}(4\tau)+\theta_{2}(2\tau)^{2}\theta_{3}(4\tau)\theta_{2}(4\tau)\right)\right\}.\end{split}

Upon an
S
-transformation, we obtain the twisted function
Z^{\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}}(\tau)=Z_{\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}}\left(-1/\tau\right)
in the form:

\begin{split}Z^{\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}}(\tau)&=\frac{\langle{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}\rangle}{32\overline{\eta(\tau)^{6}}\eta(\tau)^{2}\eta(\tau/2)}\left\{\left(\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{6}+\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{6}\right)\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)^{2}+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)^{2}\right)\right.\\
&\left.+\left(\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{2}\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{4}+\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{2}\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{4}\right)\left[\left(\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)^{2}+\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)^{2}\right)+\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.+2\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{4}\right)\right)\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)^{2}\right)\right]\right\},\\
\end{split}

whose expansion in
q
and
\bar{q}
and is:

\begin{split}Z^{\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}}(\tau)&=\frac{\langle\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}\rangle}{q^{1/8}\overline{q}^{1/4}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+5q^{1/2}+\frac{47q}{2}+75q^{3/2}+\frac{403q^{2}}{2}+501q^{5/2}+1158q^{3}+2502q^{7/2}+\frac{10309}{2}q^{4}+\right.\right.\\
&\left.+10228q^{9/2}+O\left(q^{5}\right)\right)+\overline{q}^{1/2}\left(6+32q^{1/4}+128q^{3/8}+60q^{1/2}+192q^{3/4}+512q^{7/8}+282q+\right.\\
&\left.+672q^{5/4}+1792q^{11/8}+900q^{3/2}+1984q^{7/4}+5120q^{15/8}+O\left(q^{2}\right)\right)+\overline{q}\left(33+128q^{1/4}+512q^{3/8}+\right.\\
&\left.\left.+330q^{1/2}+768q^{3/4}+2048q^{7/8}+1551q+2688q^{5/4}+7168q^{11/8}+O\left(q^{3/2}\right)\right)+O\left(\bar{q}^{3/2}\right)\right]\\
\end{split}

The minimal integer value required for the quantum dimension of
\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}
in order to have just positive integer coefficients in this expansion is
\langle\mathcal{L}^{(34)(56)}\rangle=2
. The three additional simple defects, obtained by fusion with
{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{3}},{\cal L}_{t_{2}t_{5}}
and
{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{5}}
, act on the representations
\Omega_{i}
through the operators
\pm 2P^{i}_{(34)(56)}
, where the minus sign appears for
i=2,3
, or
i=2,5
, or
i=3,5
, respectively. In all such cases, the
q
-expansion of the resulting
{\cal L}
-twisted partition functions have positive integer coefficients, as expected.

Because the coefficients
\alpha_{i}
are real and
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
has order two, we find that
{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal L}
is unoriented, and

(C.21)

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{2}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{4}}+{\cal L}_{t_{5}t_{6}}+{\cal L}_{t_{3}t_{4}t_{5}t_{6}}\ .

This implies that
{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}
coincides with the duality defect
\mathcal{N}_{34,56}\equiv{\cal L}^{(34)(56)}
related to the fact that the theory is self-orbifold with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
generated by
t_{3}t_{4}
and
t_{5}t_{6}
. Similarly, for every choice of pairwise distinct
i,j,k,l\in\{2,\ldots,6\}
, we have duality defects
\mathcal{N}_{ij,kl}\equiv\mathcal{N}_{kl,ij}
of dimension
2
such that

(C.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{ij,kl}^{2}={\cal I}+{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}}+{\cal L}_{t_{k}t_{l}}+{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}t_{k}t_{l}}\ .

Notice that, in a suitable description of the model
\mathcal{C}_{GTVW}
as a torus orbifold
T^{4}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, the symmetries
t_{i}t_{j}
,
t_{k}t_{l}
and
t_{i}t_{j}t_{k}t_{l}
can be identified, respectively, with
\eta_{\frac{\lambda}{2}}
,
Q\eta_{\frac{\lambda}{2}}
, and
Q
, where
\eta_{\frac{\lambda}{2}}
is induced by the half-periods along one of the directions of
T^{4}
, and
Q
is the quantum symmetry of the orbifold, see sections
4.2
. This suggests that
\mathcal{N}_{ij,kl}
can be identified with the topological defect
T_{\frac{\lambda}{4}}
in eq.(
4.19
).

Partition
1+5
.
Finally, let us consider the class of defects that act by the automorphism

(C.23)

\displaystyle\rho_{L}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\Omega\,\Omega^{{\dagger}}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(23645)\ ,\qquad\rho_{R}=1

of order
5
, preserving the
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
supercurrents (in our conventions, the permutation acts after the
SU(2)^{6}
transformation). The only possible for a simple defect
{\cal L}^{(23645)}
in this class is as follows (we set
{\cal L}\equiv{\cal L}^{(23645)}
in this part)

(C.24)

\displaystyle\hat{\cal L}=\langle{\cal L}\rangle P_{(23645)}\ ,

where
P_{(23645)}
acts by the automorphism
(\rho_{L},\rho_{R})
on the four NS-NS representations

[000000;000000],\ [111111;000000],\ [000000;111111],\ [111111;111111]

that satisfy

(C.25)

\displaystyle a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{4}=a_{5}=a_{6}\ ,\qquad b_{2}=b_{3}=b_{4}=b_{5}=b_{6}

and annihilates any other representation in the NS-NS sector. The characters contributing to torus NS-NS partition function are:

(C.26)

\displaystyle Z_{{\cal L}^{(23645)}}(\tau)=\langle{\cal L}\rangle\sum_{a,b=0}^{1}\ch_{a}(\tau,0)\ch_{a}(5\tau,0)\overline{\ch_{b}(\tau,0)}^{6}\ ,

which expressions in terms of the
su(2)
characters is:

\begin{split}Z_{{\cal L}^{(23645)}}(\tau)=&\langle{\cal L}\rangle\frac{1}{\overline{\eta(\tau)^{6}}}\frac{1}{\eta(\tau)\eta(5\tau)}\left(\overline{\theta_{3}(2\tau)}^{6}+\overline{\theta_{2}(2\tau)}^{6}\right)\left(\theta_{3}(2\tau)\theta_{3}(10\tau)+\theta_{2}(2\tau)\theta_{2}(10\tau)\right)\end{split}

The twined partition function, obtained from the previous one by modular transformation, takes the form:

Z^{{\cal L}^{(23645)}}(\tau)=\frac{\langle{\cal L}\rangle}{16\overline{\eta(\tau)}^{6}\eta(\tau)\eta(\tau/5)}\left(\overline{\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{6}+\overline{\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)}^{6}\right)\left(\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{3}\left(\frac{\tau}{10}\right)+\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{2}\right)\theta_{4}\left(\frac{\tau}{10}\right)\right),

with corresponding series expansion given by:

\begin{split}&Z^{{\cal L}^{(23645)}}(\tau)=\frac{\langle{\cal L}\rangle}{q^{1/20}\overline{q}^{1/4}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3}{4}q^{1/5}+q^{3/10}+q^{2/5}+q^{1/2}+\frac{7}{4}q^{3/5}+3q^{7/10}+\frac{13}{4}q^{4/5}+4q^{9/10}+\frac{11}{2}q+\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.+7q^{11/10}+\frac{19}{2}q^{6/5}+11q^{13/10}+\frac{55q^{7/5}}{4}q^{7/5}+18q^{3/2}+\frac{83}{4}q^{8/5}+26q^{17/10}+\frac{129}{4}q^{9/5}+39q^{19/10}+O\left(q^{2}\right)\right)+\right.\\
&\left.+\overline{q}^{1/2}\left(15+45q^{1/5}+60q^{3/10}+60q^{2/5}+60q^{1/2}+105q^{3/5}+180q^{7/10}+195q^{4/5}+240q^{9/10}+O\left(q\right)\right)+\right.\\
&\left.+\overline{q}\left(\frac{129}{2}+\frac{387}{2}q^{1/5}+258q^{3/10}+258q^{2/5}+258q^{1/2}+\frac{903}{2}q^{3/5}+774q^{7/10}+\frac{1677}{2}q^{4/5}+O\left(q^{9/10}\right)\right)+O(\overline{q}^{3/2})\right]\\
\end{split}

From such expansion we can extract the condition for the minimal value of the quantum dimension at
\langle{\cal L}\rangle=4
.

The product
{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}
must act trivially on the whole chiral and antichiral algebra and annihilate any representation that does not satisfy (
C.25
). Because the defect with smallest quantum dimension satisfying these properties has dimension
16
, this implies
\langle{\cal L}\rangle=4
and

(C.27)

\displaystyle{\cal L}{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal I}+\sum_{2\leq i<j\leq 6}{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}}+\sum_{i=2}^{6}{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}t_{5}t_{6}}\ .

The defects
{\cal L}^{(26534)}
,
{\cal L}^{(24356)}
,
{\cal L}^{(25463)}\equiv{\cal L}^{\star}
can be obtained by conjugation
{\cal L}_{g}{\cal L}^{(23645)}{\cal L}_{g}^{\star}
with suitable invertible defects
{\cal L}_{g}
, with
g\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
, so that all such defects have dimension
4
, and are the unique simple defects acting with the given automorphism on the chiral algebra.

Let
p_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{8}:M_{20}
be the order
2
symmetry acting by
p_{2}=(\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,;\ \mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1}\,\mathsf{1})(35)(46)
, so that
{\cal L}^{\star}={\cal L}_{p_{2}}{\cal L}{\cal L}_{p_{2}}
. Then
({\cal L}_{p_{2}}{\cal L})^{\star}={\cal L}_{p_{2}}{\cal L}
is unoriented, has dimension
4
, and

(C.28)

\displaystyle({\cal L}_{p_{2}}{\cal L})^{2}={\cal I}+\sum_{2\leq i<j\leq 6}{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{j}}+\sum_{i=2}^{6}{\cal L}_{t_{i}t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}t_{5}t_{6}}\ .

This means that
\mathcal{N}_{23456}:={\cal L}_{p_{2}}{\cal L}
is the duality defect related to the fact that the theory is self-orbifold under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{4}
group of symmetries generated by
t_{i}t_{j}
,
2\leq i<j\leq 6
.

Appendix D
The
\mathcal{N}=2

c=1
superconformal algebra as a free boson.

The
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal algebra at central charge
c_{k}=\frac{3k}{k+2}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, can be described in terms of a coset
\frac{\widehat{su}(2)_{k}\oplus\widehat{u}(1)_{4}}{\widehat{u}(1)_{2k+4}}
, which provides the bosonic subalgebra of
\mathcal{N}=2
. In the particular case of
k=1
, the bosonic subalgebra of the
\mathcal{N}=2
algebra, simplifies and becomes essentially the
\hat{u}(1)_{12}
algebra
[
93
,
94
,
95
]
.
Here,
\hat{u}(1)_{2l}
denotes
10
10

10

Our normalization differs by a factor
2
from the conventions in
[
96
]
: the algebra
\hat{u}(1)_{2l}
in this paper is denoted as
\hat{u}(1)_{l}
in
[
96
]
.
the
c=1
chiral algebra generated by a single chiral free boson
i\partial X_{L}(z)=\sum_{n}\alpha_{n}z^{-n-1}
(whose modes generate the Heisenberg algebra), together with the holomorphic vertex operators
V_{n\sqrt{2l}}(z)\sim:e^{in\sqrt{2l}X_{L}(z)}:
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, of
\alpha_{0}
-eigenvalue
n\sqrt{2l}
and conformal weight
n^{2}l
. In other words, this is the lattice VOA associated with the lattice
\sqrt{2l}\mathbb{Z}
. The irreducible representations
M^{l}_{[x]}
of
\hat{u}(1)_{2l}
are labeled by
[x]\in\mathbb{Z}/2l\mathbb{Z}
, and the characters are given by

(D.1)

\displaystyle K^{2l}_{[x]}(\tau,z)=\frac{\sum_{Q\in\frac{x}{2l}+\mathbb{Z}}q^{lQ^{2}}y^{Q}}{\eta(\tau)}\ ,\qquad y=e^{2\pi iz}\ ,

where
Q
is the
\alpha_{0}
-eigenvalue divided by
\sqrt{2l}
(this strange normalization is justified below). In particular, the ground state of
M^{l}_{[x]}
has conformal weight
h=\frac{x^{2}}{4l}
and charge
Q=\frac{x}{2l}
, where
x\in\mathbb{Z}
is a representative of
[x]\in\mathbb{Z}/2l\mathbb{Z}
in the range
-l+1\leq x\leq l
.

The
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal algebra at
c=1
is obtained by adjoining the bosonic algebra
\hat{u}(1)_{12}
(i.e.,
l=6
) with its module
M^{6}_{[6]}
, which contains the two supercurrents and the other fermionic fields. The charge
Q
is the
U(1)
R-charge with the standard normalization such that the supercurrents have charge
\pm 1/2
. In general, the
\mathcal{N}=2
modules are given by sums
M^{6}_{[x]}\oplus M^{6}_{[x+6]}
of
\hat{u}(1)_{12}
modules, and the representations are NS or Ramond depending on whether
[x]
is even or odd, respectively.
In other words, the
\mathcal{N}=2
algebra at
c=1
can be identified with the lattice SVOA related with the
odd
lattice
\sqrt{3}\mathbb{Z}
. The representations of this superalgebra are given by
R_{[x]}=M^{6}_{[x]}\oplus M^{6}_{[x+6]}
where now
x\in\mathbb{Z}/6\mathbb{Z}
, and correspond to the lattice cosets
\frac{x}{2\sqrt{3}}+\sqrt{3}\mathbb{Z}\subset\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}\mathbb{Z}
.

Gepner models are usually described in terms of the coset algebra
(\hat{su}(2)_{1}\oplus\hat{u}(1)_{4})/\hat{u}(1)_{6}
, whose representations are labeled as
[l,m,s]\equiv[1-l,m+3,s+2]
,
l=0,1
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}/6\mathbb{Z}
,
s\in\mathbb{Z}/4\mathbb{Z}
with
l+m+s\equiv 0\mod 2
. As mentioned above, the algebra
(\hat{su}(2)_{1}\oplus\hat{u}(1)_{4})/\hat{u}(1)_{6}
is isomorphic to
\hat{u}(1)_{12}
, and the respective representations can be identified as follows:

\displaystyle[0,m,0]\quad\equiv\quad M^{6}_{[m]}\qquad m\in\{0,\pm 2\}\ ,

\displaystyle[0,m,2]\quad\equiv\quad M^{6}_{[m+6]}\qquad m\in\{0,\pm 2\}

\displaystyle[0,m,1]\quad\equiv\quad M^{6}_{[m]}\qquad m\in\{3,\pm 1\}\ ,

\displaystyle[0,m,-1]\quad\equiv\quad M^{6}_{[m+6]}\qquad m\in\{3,\pm 1\}\ .

In terms of representations of the
\mathcal{N}=2
superconformal algebra, one has the following identifications

\displaystyle[0,m,0]\oplus[0,m,2]\quad\equiv\quad R_{[m]}\qquad m\in\{0,\pm 2\}\ ,

\displaystyle[0,m,1]\oplus[0,m,-1]\quad\equiv\quad R_{[m]}\qquad m\in\{3,\pm 1\}\ .
